+# ==========================================================================
+# Inc 2b - the encode side (G-T1).  Rows R-A2L-DEF.19/6-/7, .25/2, .26/2-/4,
+# .40 ... .57, .138, .152, .151/7, the waiting encode arms of .21/3, .58.1,
+# .58.2 and AT-B103-I2-1 (AT-B103-I2-2 lives in test_b103_synth_definitions.py).
+# ``encode_definition`` / ``Encoded`` / ``Definition.shared_with`` are I-2's
+# write side; expected bytes, codes and messages are the requirements' exact
+# texts, and the V171 overlap names are re-derived through the current parser
+# (evidence-e4/e4-2b-probe.py), never from the old census.
+# ==========================================================================
+
+
+def _enc(d: Definition, a2l: dict, text: str, image: Mapping[int, int]):
+    return encode_definition(d, a2l, text, image)
+
+
+def _clear(d: Definition) -> Definition:
+    """The .43/.44/.45/.46/.48/.53/.56 V171 cases clear ``shared_with`` only as a
+    test device; a consumer never clears it and re-describes after an A2L change."""
+    return dataclasses.replace(d, shared_with=None)
+
+
+def _ref(code: str, message: str) -> Refusal:
+    return Refusal(code, message)
+
+
+def _first_of(c08: dict, conv: str, dt: str | None = None) -> dict:
+    return next(o for o in c08["objs"] if o["conversion"] == conv and (dt is None or o["datatype"] == dt))
+
+
+def _c08_with_format(c08: dict, tmp_path: Path, name: str, fmt: str) -> dict:
+    """Case 08's A2L with one FORMAT line added to the named MEASUREMENT."""
+    src = (c08["dir"] / "firmware.a2l").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+    pattern = rf'(/begin MEASUREMENT {name} "synthetic measurement"\n)(\s+[^\n]+\n)'
+    new = re.sub(pattern, lambda m: m.group(1) + m.group(2) + f'      FORMAT "{fmt}"\n', src, count=1)
+    assert new != src, name
+    path = tmp_path / f"c08_fmt_{name}.a2l"
+    path.write_text(new, encoding="utf-8")
+    return parse_a2l_file(path)
+
+
+# ---- S2.7a residuals (.19/6, .19/7) ------------------------------------------------------
+
+def test_r_a2l_def_19_6_second_live_bit_mask_keyword_is_refused(tmp_path):
+    """R-A2L-DEF.19/6 (Security F1) - two live BIT_MASK lines refuse A2L-DEF-MASK."""
+    a2l = _inline(tmp_path, _val("MP", "0x10", "RL_U16", "NO_COMPU_METHOD", "0", "65535",
+                                 "BIT_MASK 0x000F", "BIT_MASK 0x000F"))
+    d = describe_definition("MP", a2l)
+    assert d.refusal == _ref("A2L-DEF-MASK", "MP: the bit mask 0x000F cannot be applied to UWORD")
+    assert encode_definition(d, a2l, "7", {0x10: 0, 0x11: 0}) == d.refusal
+
+
+def test_r_a2l_def_19_7_ambiguous_byte_order_source_is_refused(tmp_path):
+    """R-A2L-DEF.19/7 (E3S-36) - a masked definition whose MOD_COMMON holds two live BYTE_ORDER keywords."""
+    mod_common = "\n".join(['    /begin MOD_COMMON ""', "      BYTE_ORDER MSB_FIRST",
+                            "      BYTE_ORDER MSB_LAST", "    /end MOD_COMMON"])
+    a2l = _inline(tmp_path, mod_common,
+                  _val("BO", "0x10", "RL_U16", "NO_COMPU_METHOD", "0", "65535", "BIT_MASK 0x0FF0"),
+                  byte_order=None)
+    d = describe_definition("BO", a2l)
+    assert d.refusal == _ref("A2L-DEF-MASK", "BO: its byte order cannot be read")
+
+
+# ---- R-A2L-DEF.25/2 (encode gives the definition's refusal) ----------------------------------
+
+def test_r_a2l_def_25_2_encode_gives_the_definitions_refusal(c08):
+    """R-A2L-DEF.25/2 - the encode of a refused definition returns that refusal, no bytes."""
+    d = describe_definition("MISSING_CM", c08["issues"])
+    assert d.refusal == _ref("A2L-DEF-METHOD", "MISSING_CM: the conversion method CM_MISSING is not declared in the A2L")

+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# Inc 2b (G-T2) - the write arm of AT-B103-I2-2 and the DEFC masked writes
+# (.138/16, .46/3).  Rows .46, .48, .55, .56, .138.  Expected bytes from the
+# graph's AT-B103-I2-2 / .138/16 examples over the seed-20260001 word 0x1AE7.
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+_SCALAR_DEFC = ("ENUM_0001", "MASK_BIT_0001", "MASK_FIELD_0001", "DISPLAY_0001")
+
+
+def test_r_a2l_def_138_16_two_disjoint_masks_still_encode(defc):
+    """R-A2L-DEF.138/16 - DEFC's two disjoint masks of one word both encode, no OVERLAP."""
+    m = _model()
+    image = defc["mem"]
+    bit = m.describe_definition("MASK_BIT_0001", defc["a2l"])
+    field = m.describe_definition("MASK_FIELD_0001", defc["a2l"])
+    assert bit.shared_with is None and field.shared_with is None
+    assert m.encode_definition(bit, defc["a2l"], "0", image) == m.Encoded(0x800010, bytes([0x1A, 0xE6]), ())
+    assert m.encode_definition(field, defc["a2l"], "3", image) == m.Encoded(0x800010, bytes([0x10, 0x37]), ())
+
+
+def test_r_a2l_def_46_3_two_fields_of_one_big_endian_word(defc):
+    """R-A2L-DEF.46/3 - write the multi-bit field, then decode both masked objects over the applied image."""
+    m = _model()
+    field = m.describe_definition("MASK_FIELD_0001", defc["a2l"])
+    bit = m.describe_definition("MASK_BIT_0001", defc["a2l"])
+    res = m.encode_definition(field, defc["a2l"], "3", defc["mem"])
+    assert isinstance(res, m.Encoded)
+    new_image = dict(defc["mem"])
+    new_image.update({0x800010 + i: b for i, b in enumerate(res.data)})
+    assert m.decode_definition(field, defc["a2l"], new_image).text == "3"
+    assert m.decode_definition(bit, defc["a2l"], new_image).text == "1"
+
+
+def test_at_b103_i2_2_write_arm_encodes_planned_then_a_second_value(defc):
+    """AT-B103-I2-2 (2b arm) - encode each DEFC scalar's planned text, apply, then a second value.
+
+    ENUM_0001 IDLE -> FAULT (03); MASK_FIELD_0001 174 -> 3 (word 0x1037) with MASK_BIT_0001
+    still 1; MASK_BIT_0001 1 -> 0 (0x1036); DISPLAY_0001 143.5 -> -12.5 (FF E7); every other
+    byte of the region unchanged.
+    """
+    m = _model()
+    second = {"ENUM_0001": "FAULT", "MASK_BIT_0001": "0", "MASK_FIELD_0001": "3", "DISPLAY_0001": "-12.5"}
+    base_image = defc["mem"]
+    image = dict(base_image)
+    for name in _SCALAR_DEFC:
+        d = m.describe_definition(name, defc["a2l"])
+        assert d.shared_with is None, (name, d.shared_with)
+        planned = m.decode_definition(d, defc["a2l"], image)
+        assert isinstance(planned, m.Decoded), (name, planned)
+        res = m.encode_definition(d, defc["a2l"], planned.text, image)
+        assert isinstance(res, m.Encoded), (name, res)
+        image.update({d.address + i: b for i, b in enumerate(res.data)})
+        assert m.decode_definition(d, defc["a2l"], image).text == planned.text, name
+        res2 = m.encode_definition(d, defc["a2l"], second[name], image)
+        assert isinstance(res2, m.Encoded), (name, res2)
+        image.update({d.address + i: b for i, b in enumerate(res2.data)})
+        assert m.decode_definition(d, defc["a2l"], image).text == second[name], name
+    # the four scalars' final bytes, everything else untouched
+    assert image[0x800000] == 0x03
+    assert (image[0x800010], image[0x800011]) == (0x10, 0x36)
+    assert (image[0x800020], image[0x800021]) == (0xFF, 0xE7)
+    written = {0x800000, 0x800010, 0x800011, 0x800020, 0x800021}
+    for addr, b in base_image.items():
+        if addr not in written:
+            assert image[addr] == b, hex(addr)

Document in the report.

  - `.58.1/1` and `.58.1/2`: immutability. Same — they discriminate mutation, not wrong results. The negative control must be a mutation; the stub didn't mutate. Document.

  - `AT-B103-I2-1 write arm`: this one IS discriminating — it asserts exact OVERLAP refusals for 9 objects + ASCII round trip. Under a behavioral stub that "leaves every expected result wrong", it passed. That means the stub must return the definition's refusal when d.refusal is set... no wait — for the 9 overlap objects, d.refusal is None (the refusal happens at encode). Hmm, unless the stub computes overlap refusals. OR — maybe the stub passes through encode to a real... no, there's no real encode in the base.

  Hmm wait, actually maybe the stub replaced `s19_app.tui.a2l_definition` with a module where encode_definition returns a wrong Encoded (e.g., empty bytes) UNLESS d.shared_with is set, in which case it returns the overlap refusal? Or the stub could raise/return wrong. Without the stub source I can't tell. Alternatively the previous tester designed the stub to leave "every expected result wrong" only for the value-producing paths, and implemented the encode contract minimally (definition refusal pass-through, overlap refusal, text encode) — no.

  Actually — wait. Maybe the stub isn't a from-scratch module. The import in the stub run worked (`from s19_app.tui.a2l_definition import Encoded` succeeded — tests ran). The base tree has no `Encoded`/`encode_definition`. The stub tree /tmp/b103-e4-2b-stub added them. Possibly the stub = copy of the REAL implementation from the implementation branch (cb083d07) with... no — then wrong results wouldn't occur; but 113 tests failed. 

  OR the stub was: `encode_definition(d, ...)` returns `Refusal("A2L-DEF-STUB", ...)` when d.refusal is None... then AT-I2-1 would fail on ASCII encode (res must be Encoded). It passed, so ASCII encode worked correctly under the stub.

  Alternatively — the AT-I2-1 test under the stub: the 9 overlap asserts `_enc(d, ...) == _ref("A2L-DEF-OVERLAP", ...)`. For this to pass, the stub's encode must produce the exact overlap refusal text including the shared object names. That's real logic. Suspicious.

  Unless... the AT-I2-1 test DIDN'T run in the stub transcript? It's in the file (line 3480) and the stub run was against the same tree. The FAILED list has 113 entries; new tests = 119; 6 not in failed list: at_b103_i2_1, 58_1_1, 58_1_2, 58_2_12, 58_2_14, 58_2_15. So yes AT-I2-1 passed under the stub.

  Hmm — OR the stub was built from the implementation branch encode_definition (real code!) but with a twist making results "wrong": e.g., stub = real encode but with a deliberate systematic error like... but then overlap refusals would also be wrong...

  Wait, maybe not: if the stub is the real encode_definition PLUS something wrong on the numeric paths only? E.g., returns bytes+1? Then .41/1 (bytes 07 expected, got 08) fails; overlap paths untouched (they return refusals before byte generation) → AT-I2-1's nine refusal asserts pass; ASCII "ABC" text path... if the corruption applied only to number encodes, ASCII passes too. That's plausible: the stub corrupted the number conversion (e.g., stored value + 1 or byte reversal) but kept refusal logic.

  If so, AT-I2-1's RED is only for the refusal arms, and its ASCII encode arm is not discriminated by the stub. Its numeric-refusal parts are exercised by other tests anyway. The brief: "No vacuous assertions: every negative control must identify an observable wrong result." The stub is the negative control. For arms the stub can't make wrong (pure refusal pass-throughs... no wait, the nine refusals ARE produced by the stub and are correct → the test does NOT identify a wrong result on those arms → vacuous under the stub).

  Hmm. But the refusal messages are computed by the encode-side overlap logic — if the stub implements overlap logic itself, then testing exact refusal texts against the stub is circular?? No — the stub is throwaway; the point of stub RED is to show tests fail when behavior is wrong. If the stub produces CORRECT refusals, the test passing tells nothing.

  So to be rigorous, AT-I2-1's encode arms need RED via a different control. This is getting deep. Given I can't access the runner, I cannot produce new runs at all. The honest path:

  1. Do all local work: completeness verification, doc columns, report — and in the report, flag exactly these 6 arms and what's needed (a runner re-run with a corrected stub that also corrupts refusal paths, or accept the collection RED + real-base RED as the RED for those arms).

#!/bin/bash
set +e
BASE=/home/<user>/work/b103-inc2b-tests
STUB=/tmp/b103-e4-2b-stub
PY=/home/<user>/miniconda3/envs/s19ci/bin/python
rm -rf /tmp/b103e4-2b-home && mkdir -p /tmp/b103e4-2b-home
export HOME=/tmp/b103e4-2b-home
export XDG_CACHE_HOME=/tmp/b103e4-2b-home/.cache
export XDG_CONFIG_HOME=/tmp/b103e4-2b-home/.config
export LOCALAPPDATA=/tmp/b103e4-2b-home/.local
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
OUT=$BASE/evidence_out
mkdir -p "$OUT"

cd "$BASE"
echo "# red-collection transcript $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ) host <runner>-rtx" > "$OUT/2b-red-collection.txt"
echo "# command: $PY -I -B run_pytest_runner.py -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_b103_a2l_definition.py tests/test_b103_synth_definitions.py" >> "$OUT/2b-red-collection.txt"
$PY -I -B run_pytest_runner.py -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_b103_a2l_definition.py tests/test_b103_synth_definitions.py < /dev/null >> "$OUT/2b-red-collection.txt" 2>&1
echo "# exit $?" >> "$OUT/2b-red-collection.txt"

echo "# red-synth-real transcript $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" > "$OUT/2b-red-synth-real.txt"
$PY -I -B run_pytest_runner.py -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_b103_synth_definitions.py < /dev/null >> "$OUT/2b-red-synth-real.txt" 2>&1
echo "# exit $?" >> "$OUT/2b-red-synth-real.txt"

cd "$STUB"
echo "# red-stub-main transcript $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" > "$OUT/2b-red-stub-main.txt"
$PY -I -B run_pytest_runner.py -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_b103_a2l_definition.py < /dev/null >> "$OUT/2b-red-stub-main.txt" 2>&1
echo "# exit $?" >> "$OUT/2b-red-stub-main.txt"

echo "# red-stub-synth transcript $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" > "$OUT/2b-red-stub-synth.txt"
$PY -I -B run_pytest_runner.py -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_b103_synth_definitions.py < /dev/null >> "$OUT/2b-red-stub-synth.txt" 2>&1
echo "# exit $?" >> "$OUT/2b-red-stub-synth.txt"

echo "=== summaries ==="
for f in 2b-red-collection 2b-red-synth-real 2b-red-stub-main 2b-red-stub-synth; do
  echo "--- $f ---"
  tail -5 "$OUT/$f.txt"
done

  1. Add `_clear_parse_errors` helper next to `_clear` (in the 2b section near line 2415):

  ```python
  def _clear_parse_errors(a2l: dict) -> dict:
      """A copy of the payload with only the parse-error list cleared.

      The pinned case 08 payload carries parse errors (the VAL_BLK size notes), and
      R-A2L-DEF.152 refuses every encode while one is present; the conversion, limits,
      precision and round-trip rows are exercised on the clean copy (the objects are
      unchanged).  Mirrors .138/13's isolation of the overlap oracle from .152.
      """
      clean = copy.deepcopy(a2l)
      clean["errors"] = []
      return clean
  ```
